down that I missed something and one of my clients got hurt because of me, like they are just not telling me yet.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When I'm alone in the car or in a quiet client's house, I hear a voice clear as somebody standing beside me saying I failed them and should be punished, but when I look nobody is there.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, maybe a little bit? I hope it's okay to admit that. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm a bad person. 

I just... lately, I can't stop thinking about every little mistake I've ever made. Like, things I did wrong with my clients, or even with my family. I try so hard to be good at my job and take care of everyone, but sometimes I just feel like I'm completely worthless as an aide. And... well, sometimes it feels like God is just really mad at me because I haven't done a better job of protecting people. 

I know that probably sounds terrible, and I promise I'm still trying my best every single day. I just get these thoughts in my head and they make me feel so awful. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression of me. I really do care about my clients.

I just kind of snap awake and the fear is already there, but my head is just blank about what I was actually dreaming about. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't remember. I'm trying really hard to give you all the right pieces so you can do your job. Is that still helpful for you?\n\nDoctor: Is the fear you experience when you wake up related to something specific, like a person, place, or thing?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I'm so sorry, I know that's probably not very helpful for you. It's not tied to a specific person or, like, a place or anything. It's just... I wake up and the fear is just there, all on its own. \n\nI guess it's probably just me being a little silly, or maybe just letting the stress of work get to me? I really don't want to make a huge deal out of it if it's just normal stress. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better reason for it... am I doing okay explaining things so far?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this disrupted sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's been a little while now. I probably should have figured out how to handle it by myself before now, I'm sorry. \n\nIt's been going on for about a year and two months. It started right around the time my agency, um, they changed my schedule so I had to do rotating overnight shifts. And they gave me a bunch of extra paperwork on top of that, too. \n\nI really just thought it was me not adjusting well enough to the new hours, you know? Like I just needed to try harder to get used to it. I didn't want to complain or be a bother to anyone at work about the changes. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for. I've kind of pulled away from church. And I stopped going to Zumba and doing the mentoring I used to do. I just... I can barely manage to get through my shifts and get home, and then I just don't have anything left in me. I feel so lazy even saying that out loud, I'm sorry. It started right around the time my agency, um, they changed my schedule so I had to do rotating overnight shifts. Like I just needed to try harder to get used to it. I didn't want to complain or be a bother to anyone at work about the changes. \n\nDoes that make sense? Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time. \n\nI guess I came in because, well, my sleep is just a little broken up right now. It's probably nothing, really, just my schedule? I work overnight shifts doing personal care, but... lately, I get off work and I still just lie there wide awake. And then, um... when I do finally drift off, I keep waking up feeling really scared. And I just feel like I never rested at all. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel scared before you fall asleep or is the fear something you experience after you wake up?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, I'm sorry if I didn't explain that very well. It's mostly after I wake up. \n\nWhen I first get home from my shift, I just... I just lie there wide awake for a long time. I don't think I'm scared then, just really awake, if that makes sense? But then, like I mentioned, when I finally do manage to drift off for a little bit, I wake up suddenly and just feel terrified. And because of that, I just feel like I never rested at all. \n\nIs that the kind of detail you needed? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember having any dreams or thoughts right before you wake up feeling terrified?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I'm so sorry. I wish I could give you a better answer. \n\nI don't really remember having any bad dreams or... or specific thoughts right before it happens. I just kind of snap awake and the fear is already there, but my head is just blank about what I was actually dreaming about. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't remember. I'm trying really hard to give you all the right pieces so you can do your job. Is that still helpful for you?\n\nDoctor: Is the fear you experience when you wake up related to something specific, like a person, place, or thing?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I'm so sorry, I know that's probably not very helpful for you. It's not tied to a specific person or, like, a place or anything. It's just... I wake up and the fear is just there, all on its own. \n\nI guess it's probably just me being a little silly, or maybe just letting the stress of work get to me? I really don't want to make a huge deal out of it if it's just normal stress. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better reason for it... am I doing okay explaining things so far?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this disrupted sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's been a little while now. I probably should have figured out how to handle it by myself before now, I'm sorry. \n\nIt's been going on for about a year and two months. It started right around the time my agency, um, they changed my schedule so I had to do rotating overnight shifts. And they gave me a bunch of extra paperwork on top of that, too. \n\nI really just thought it was me not adjusting well enough to the new hours, you know? Like I just needed to try harder to get used to it. I didn't want to complain or be a bother to anyone at work about the changes. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for. Let me know if you need me to be more exact!\n\nDoctor: Has this sleep pattern been affecting your ability to perform your job or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, I really hate to admit this, I'm so sorry. I know I should just push through it better, but... yeah, I guess it has been affecting things. \n\nI just... I can't seem to keep up with my care visits safely anymore. It's so embarrassing, but my supervisor actually had to stop me right in the middle of a care-plan visit recently. I just got so overwhelmed and, like, stuck... I couldn't finish it. I felt awful making her step in to take over. I really hope she doesn't think I'm a bad aide, because I really do care about my clients. \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I've kind of pulled away from church. And I stopped going to Zumba and doing the mentoring I used to do. I just... I can barely manage to get through my shifts and get home, and then I just don't have anything left in me. I feel so lazy even saying that out loud, I'm sorry. I'm really trying my best to keep it together, I promise. \n\nIs... is it okay that I told you that? Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Does the fear you experience when you wake up stay with you for a while after you're awake, or does it go away pretty quickly?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's a little hard to say, honestly. I'm sorry, I wish I had a clearer answer for you. \n\nI guess... I mean, it usually goes away pretty quickly, I think? Once I'm up and moving around, getting ready for the day, I try to just shake it off and focus on what I need to do. I don't want to let it ruin my whole morning, you know? So I just try to push it aside and keep going. \n\nI really hope that's normal. I mean, everybody wakes up a little startled sometimes, right? I'm probably just making a mountain out of a molehill. Does that sound like a normal way to react? I hope I'm not overreacting to it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or activities throughout your day where you feel the fear or anxiety from waking up still affects you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. I'm sorry, I hope that's okay to say. \n\nLike I said, I really try to just leave it in the bedroom, you know? Once I'm at work or, well, trying to get through the day, I'm just focused on not messing up and trying to be helpful. I don't really notice the waking-up feeling sticking around when I'm doing other things. I just kind of put my head down and try to work hard. \n\nI'm sorry if that's not very helpful. Am I making sense? I just don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know I just need to be stronger about it. Is that the kind of answer you needed?\n\nDoctor: Are there any things you used to do or places you used to go that you're avoiding now because of the fear you wake up with?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. Not because of that, anyway. I mean, I know I mentioned earlier that I stopped going to church and Zumba, but... that's really just because I've been so tired and, um, just not managing my energy very well. It's not because of the waking up scared part. \n\nI really try not to let that morning feeling dictate where I go or what I do, I promise. I just push past it so I'm not, you know, making a big fuss over nothing. If I'm avoiding things, it's really just my own fault for being too worn out. \n\nUm... I hope I'm understanding your question right. Is that what you meant? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience sudden episodes of intense fear or discomfort during the day, when you're not waking up from sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I'm sorry, I hope it's okay to say no to that. \n\nI don't really get, like, sudden intense fear out of nowhere during the day. I mean, my job gets a little stressful sometimes—taking care of people can be a lot of work—but I just try to keep a smile on and push through it. I definitely don't just freak out or anything, I promise. I try really hard to keep it together so I'm doing a good job and not being a burden to anyone. \n\nI just feel the normal amount of stress, I think. I hope that makes sense? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in crowded places or social situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I hope that's the right answer. \n\nI don't really get scared in crowds or anything. I just... well, I guess I haven't been around a lot of people lately anyway, just because I've been so tired and keeping to myself. But it's not because I'm afraid of them or anxious about being in a crowd. I promise I'm not, like, afraid of people or anything weird like that. I just try to be polite and do my job when I am around others.\n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? I just don't want you to think I'm making things up or being difficult. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fear you experience when you wake up is related to anything specific that happens during your work shifts or daily routines?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think it's related to my work or anything like that. I hope it's okay to say that. I really try to do a good job with my clients, and I don't want you to think I can't handle my shifts. I promise I can, I always try my best for them. \n\nThe waking up scared part just... it just sort of happens on its own, I guess. I haven't really noticed it being connected to anything specific I do during the day or any part of my routine. \n\nI'm sorry, I wish I could give you a better reason for why it happens. I just don't really know. Am I answering your questions okay? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your appetite or interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I hope it's alright to say that. \n\nMy appetite is pretty much the same, I think. I always make sure to eat so I can keep my strength up for my clients—I really don't want to let them down by being weak or anything. \n\nAnd as for things I like doing... well, I guess I've just been resting a lot on my days off, mostly because I've been so tired lately. But I still like my normal stuff! I promise I'm not just sitting around giving up or being lazy. I still try to do things when I have the energy, I really do. \n\nDoes that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms, such as a racing heart or sweating, when you wake up feeling terrified?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really hope that's the right answer. \n\nWhen I wake up like that, I don't really notice my heart beating fast or, you know, getting all sweaty or anything like that. I just... I just feel really scared, I guess. But my body feels okay, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm having panic attacks or that I can't handle things physically. I'm actually pretty healthy, and I always make sure I'm strong enough for my work. \n\nI just... I wake up feeling that way, but without all those extra physical things you mentioned. Is that okay? I hope I'm explaining it right so it's helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fear you wake up with might be connected to something that's been on your mind or a memory that's been bothering you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, maybe a little bit? I hope it's okay to admit that. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm a bad person. \n\nI just... lately, I can't stop thinking about every little mistake I've ever made. Like, things I did wrong with my clients, or even with my family. I try so hard to be good at my job and take care of everyone, but sometimes I just feel like I'm completely worthless as an aide. And... well, sometimes it feels like God is just really mad at me because I haven't done a better job of protecting people. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I promise I'm still trying my best every single day. I just get these thoughts in my head and they make me feel so awful. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression of me. I really do care about my clients.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fear you wake up with is related to feeling guilty about something?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I guess so. I hope it's alright to say that again. \n\nI just... I keep going over every little thing I've ever done wrong, you know? With my patients, or even my family. I just feel like... like I'm a completely worthless aide, and I shouldn't be. I try so hard to be good for them. But it just feels like... like God is mad at me because I didn't protect people better. \n\nI know I sort of just said that, but that's really what it is. I wake up and I just feel so guilty for not being better. I promise I'm a hard worker, though. I really do care. I just... I hope it's okay that I feel this way. Am I answering your question right?\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you feel guilty about that you think might be contributing to the fear you wake up with?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm bad at my job, because I really do try to be so careful. \n\nBut... sometimes I just get this awful feeling that I did something wrong at work. Like, I'll turn in my care notes for the day, and my supervisor will look them over and tell me everything is perfectly fine. And I smile and say thank you, but... deep down, I just know that I missed something. \n\nI get this feeling like... like one of my clients got hurt because I wasn't paying enough attention, and everyone knows it but they're just... not telling me yet. Like they're waiting for the right time to tell me how bad I messed up. \n\nI know that probably sounds silly since my supervisor said I did fine, and I really don't want you to think I'm a negligent aide. I always double-check my work! It's just... that feeling won't go away, and I think that's why I wake up so scared. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular event or action at work that you feel guilty about and think might be contributing to your fear?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think there's just one specific time... I'm sorry, I know I'm probably not explaining this very well. I hope you're not getting frustrated with me. \n\nIt's just... kind of like what I was trying to say a minute ago. It's not one single memory I can point to, it's just this... this heavy feeling every time I finish my shift. Even when my supervisor looks right at me and says my care notes are completely fine, I just... I still know, deep down, that I missed something terrible. I just know that one of my clients got hurt because of me, and everyone else knows it too, but they're just... they're just not telling me yet. \n\nI really am a good aide, I swear. I would never hurt anyone on purpose. I just have this absolute certainty that I messed up and caused someone pain, even when they tell me I didn't. Does that make sense? I'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself, I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a traumatic event at work that might be contributing to your feelings of guilt and fear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, nothing like that. I mean, I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm hiding anything, but there hasn't been any big traumatic event or anything awful like that. \n\nI've never seen anyone get seriously hurt while I was there, and I really try to be so, so careful with all my clients. I would never want you to think I've been involved in something terrible at work. It's just... it's just that feeling I was talking about. That feeling that I *did* do something, even though nothing actually happened that I can remember. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds really confusing. I hope I'm making sense. Is that... is that the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
